{"type":"user.message","data":{"content":"\n<system_reminder>\nContents of src/providers/AGENTS.md:\n\n# Provider Implementations\n\nLLM provider integrations implementing `ApiProvider` interface to call different AI services.\n\n## Architecture\n\nEach provider:\n\n- Implements `ApiProvider` interface (`src/types/providers.ts`)\n- Transforms prompts → provider-specific API format\n- Returns normalized `ProviderResponse`\n- Handles auth, rate limits, retries, streaming\n\n## Provider Lifecycle &amp; Cleanup\n\nThe evaluator (`src/evaluator.ts`) manages provider lifecycle. After evaluation completes, it calls `providerRegistry.shutdownAll()` to clean up resources.\n\n**If your provider allocates resources** (Python workers, connections, child processes):\n\n- Implement a `cleanup()` method on your provider\n- Register with `providerRegistry` for automatic cleanup\n- Resources are released in the evaluator&apos;s `finally` block\n\n**Reference implementations:**\n\n- `openai.ts` - Most comprehensive\n- `anthropic/index.ts` - Complex provider with subdirectory\n- `http.ts` - Generic HTTP pattern\n\n## Logging\n\nSee `docs/agents/logging.md` - use logger with object context (auto-sanitized).\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n**OpenAI-compatible providers** extend `OpenAiChatCompletionProvider`. See `src/providers/quiverai.ts` for a minimal example or `src/providers/openrouter.ts` for a more complex one.\n\n**Config priority:** Explicit options &gt; Environment variables &gt; Provider defaults\n\n## Provider Routing\n\nPrefix dispatch in `src/providers/registry.ts` is case-sensitive and meaningful. Similar\nprefixes can route to different classes. When you add a new sub-type (e.g.\n`:moderation`, `:embedding`, `:realtime`) to one prefix, do one of:\n\n- Add the sub-type to every prefix it should work under, **or**\n- Explicitly fail-fast (throw with a clear message) for the prefixes that should not\n  support it.\n\nSilently mapping `foo:newtype` to a class that only handles `foo:chat` is a routing\nregression. Add a test in `test/providers/registry.test.ts` that asserts each\nprefix/sub-type pair resolves to the expected class or throws.\n\n## Cache Key Hygiene\n\nPromptfoo&apos;s disk cache lives at `${getConfigDirectoryPath()}/cache` (typically\n`~/.promptfoo/cache`), unless overridden with `PROMPTFOO_CACHE_PATH` or\n`PROMPTFOO_CONFIG_DIR`. If an implementation stores literal cache-key strings, those\nstrings persist to disk; if it stores hashed keys, the hash persists.\n\n- **Never include secrets in cache keys.** Strip `Authorization`, bearer tokens, API\n  keys, tenant tokens, signed metadata, and custom auth headers before building or\n  hashing a key. If those values materially change the response, use a non-secret\n  tenant/account identifier, a cache namespace, or disable caching for that path; do not\n  hash raw secrets into long-lived cache keys.\n- **Canonicalize before hashing.** `JSON.stringify({a, b})` and `JSON.stringify({b, a})`\n  produce different strings but represent the same config, so naïve stringification\n  causes cache misses for semantically identical requests. Sort keys (or use a\n  canonical-JSON helper) before hashing.\n- Reference pattern for stable key structure and canonicalized hashing:\n  `getModerationCacheKey` in `src/providers/azure/moderation.ts`.\n\n## Caching Best Practices\n\nWhen implementing caching in your provider, **ALWAYS set the `cached: true` flag** when returning a cached response:\n\n```typescript\n// ✅ CORRECT - Always set cached flag\nif (cachedResponse) {\n  const parsed = JSON.parse(cachedResponse as string);\n  return { ...parsed, cached: true };\n}\n\n// ❌ WRONG - Missing cached flag\nif (cachedResponse) {\n  return JSON.parse(cachedResponse as string);\n}\n```\n\n**Why this matters:**\n\n- The `cached` flag allows downstream code to skip rate limiting delays\n- Performance metrics can distinguish between fresh API calls and cache hits\n- Evaluations can accurately track which responses were served from cache\n\n**Response type compatibility:**\n\n- `ProviderResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n- `ProviderEmbeddingResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n- `ProviderModerationResponse` has `cached?: boolean`\n\n**Reference implementations:**\n\n- `bedrock/converse.ts:985` - Sets cached flag correctly\n- `pythonCompletion.ts:194` - Example with spread operator\n- `google/vertex.ts:268` - Multiple cache points handled correctly\n\n## Testing Requirements\n\n**CRITICAL: Tests must NEVER make real API calls.** Mock all HTTP requests.\n\nEvery provider needs tests in `test/providers/`:\n\n- Mock API responses using `vi.mock`\n- Test success AND error cases\n- Test rate limits, timeouts, invalid configs\n- Run with: `npx vitest run test/providers/my-provider`\n\n## Adding a Provider\n\n**All seven items are required** before a provider is complete:\n\n1. Implement `ApiProvider` interface\n2. Add env vars to `src/types/env.ts` (`ProviderEnvOverridesSchema`)\n3. Add env vars to `src/envars.ts` (if documenting in CLI help)\n4. Add tests in `test/providers/`\n5. Add docs in `site/docs/providers/&lt;provider&gt;.md`\n6. Add entry to `site/docs/providers/index.md` table (alphabetical order)\n7. Add example in `examples/&lt;provider&gt;/`\n\nAfter updating env schema, regenerate JSON schema: `npm run jsonSchema:generate`\n\n**Verify completeness:**\n\n```bash\n# Check all pieces exist\nls src/providers/myprovider.ts\ngrep -q &quot;MYPROVIDER_API_KEY&quot; src/types/env.ts &amp;&amp; echo &quot;env.ts updated&quot;\nls test/providers/myprovider.test.ts\nls site/docs/providers/myprovider.md\ngrep -q &quot;myprovider&quot; site/docs/providers/index.md &amp;&amp; echo &quot;index.md updated&quot;\nls examples/myprovider/promptfooconfig.yaml\n```\n\n**Reference existing providers** - 50+ implementations to learn from.\n\n</system_reminder>\n<system_reminder>\nContents of src/providers/CLAUDE.md:\n\n@AGENTS.md\n\n</system_reminder>","source":"instruction-discovery","interactionId":"ac55048d-6b17-4d27-88b1-3c4becbe46bc"},"id":"5d9e9fc1-f2e9-4079-a8b4-3ec138cf8069","timestamp":"2026-06-21T21:30:28.252Z","parentId":"a944713d-f573-4082-bd6e-5908328a786d"}

} });\n```\n\n- Use Vitest&apos;s mocking utilities (`vi.mock`, `vi.fn`, `vi.spyOn`)\n- Prefer shallow mocking over deep mocking\n- Mock external dependencies but not the code being tested\n- Reset mocks between tests to prevent test pollution\n\n**Critical: Mock Isolation**\n\n`vi.clearAllMocks()` only clears call history, NOT mock implementations. Use `mockReset()` for full isolation:\n\n```typescript\nbeforeEach(() =&gt; {\n  vi.clearAllMocks(); // Clears .mock.calls and .mock.results\n  vi.mocked(myMock).mockReset(); // Also clears mockReturnValue/mockResolvedValue\n});\n```\n\nFor `vi.hoisted()` mocks or mocks with `mockReturnValue()`, you MUST call `mockReset()` in `beforeEach` to ensure test isolation when tests run in random order.\n\n## Environment Variables\n\nPrefer `mockProcessEnv()` from `test/util/utils.ts` for root tests that need to change environment variables. The root hygiene suite blocks new files that use direct environment mutation because tests run in random order.\n\n## Zustand Store Testing\n\nWhen testing components that use Zustand stores, prefer **integration testing with real stores** over mocking.\n\n### When to Use Real Stores vs Mocking\n\n**Use real stores (preferred)** when:\n\n- Testing components that modify store state\n- Verifying state changes after user interactions\n- Testing store action logic (merge, update, delete)\n\n**Mock the store** only when:\n\n- Testing pure UI components that only read from stores\n- Testing isolated component logic completely unrelated to state\n\n### Pattern: Integration Testing with Real Stores\n\n**Reference**: `src/app/src/pages/redteam/setup/components/PluginsTab.test.tsx`\n\n```typescript\nimport { act, render, screen, waitFor } from &apos;@testing-library/react&apos;;\nimport userEvent from &apos;@testing-library/user-event&apos;;\nimport { beforeEach, afterEach, describe, expect, test, vi } from &apos;vitest&apos;;\nimport { useMyStore } from &apos;./useMyStore&apos;;\n\n// Capture initial state OUTSIDE describe block\nconst initialState = useMyStore.getState();\n\ndescribe(&apos;MyComponent&apos;, () =&gt; {\n  beforeEach(() =&gt; {\n    // Reset store to initial state before each test\n    act(() =&gt; {\n      useMyStore.setState(initialState);\n    });\n  });\n\n  afterEach(() =&gt; {\n    // Clean up after each test\n    act(() =&gt; {\n      useMyStore.setState(initialState);\n    });\n  });\n\n  test(&apos;user interaction updates store correctly&apos;, async () =&gt; {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n\n    // Verify initial state\n    expect(useMyStore.getState().items).toHaveLength(0);\n\n    render(&lt;MyComponent /&gt;);\n\n    await user.click(screen.getByRole(&apos;button&apos;, { name: &apos;Add Item&apos; }));\n\n    // Assert on actual store state, not mock calls\n    await waitFor(() =&gt; {\n      expect(useMyStore.getState().items).toHaveLength(1);\n    });\n  });\n});\n```\n\n### Key Patterns\n\n1. **Capture initial state outside describe**: `const initialState = useMyStore.getState();`\n2. **Reset in beforeEach AND afterEach**: Ensures test isolation\n3. **Wrap setState in act()**: Required for React state batching\n4. **Assert on getState()**: Verify actual store state, not that a mock was called\n5. **Use waitFor for async**: Store updates may be asynchronous\n6. **Mock external dependencies only**: Mock APIs, analytics, child components—NOT the store\n\n### Anti-Pattern: Mocking the Store (avoid this)\n\n```typescript\n// ❌ AVOID: Mocking the store hook loses integration coverage\nvi.mock(&apos;./useMyStore&apos;);\nconst mockUpdateItems = vi.fn();\n(useMyStore as any).mockReturnValue({\n  items: [],\n  updateItems: mockUpdateItems,\n});\n\ntest(&apos;clicking button calls updateItems&apos;, () =&gt; Comprehensive fake timer usage\n- `test/scheduler/providerRateLimitState.test.ts` - Async code with timers\n\n### Basic Pattern\n\n```typescript\nimport { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from &apos;vitest&apos;;\n\ndescribe(&apos;MyTimerCode&apos;, () =&gt; {\n  beforeEach(() =&gt; {\n    vi.useFakeTimers();\n  });\n\n  afterEach(() =&gt; {\n    vi.useRealTimers();\n  });\n\n  it(&apos;handles timeout&apos;, () =&gt; {\n    const callback = vi.fn();\n    setTimeout(callback, 1000);\n\n    vi.advanceTimersByTime(1000);\n\n    expect(callback).toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n});\n```\n\n### Async Code with Timers\n\nWhen testing async functions that internally use timers, you cannot simply `await` them—the promise will hang because fake timers don&apos;t advance automatically. Use this pattern:\n\n```typescript\nit(&apos;handles async retry with delay&apos;, async () =&gt; {\n  // 1. Start the promise (don&apos;t await yet)\n  const promise = myAsyncFunctionWithRetry();\n\n  // 2. Run all timers to completion\n  await vi.runAllTimersAsync();\n\n  // 3. Now await the result\n  const result = await promise;\n\n  expect(result).toBe(&apos;success&apos;);\n});\n```\n\n### Why Fake Timers Matter\n\nReal timers cause flaky tests because:\n\n- **Timer resolution varies**: Windows has ~15ms minimum resolution vs ~1-4ms on Linux/Mac\n- **Race conditions**: Code checking `Date.now()` multiple times can see different values if a millisecond boundary is crossed\n- **CI variability**: Tests may pass locally but fail under CI load\n\n**Anti-pattern** (causes flaky tests):\n\n```typescript\n// ❌ AVOID: Real timers with small delays\nit(&apos;retries after delay&apos;, async () =&gt; {\n  const result = await functionThatRetries({ retryAfterMs: 1 }); // May timeout randomly\n});\n```\n\n**Correct pattern**:\n\n```typescript\n// ✓ CORRECT: Fake timers with controlled advancement\nit(&apos;retries after delay&apos;, async () =&gt; {\n  vi.useFakeTimers();\n  const promise = functionThatRetries({ retryAfterMs: 1000 });\n  await vi.runAllTimersAsync();\n  const result = await promise;\n  vi.useRealTimers();\n});\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n- Ensure all tests are independent and can run in any order\n- Clean up any test data or mocks after each test\n- Run the full test suite before committing changes\n- Test failures should be deterministic\n- For database tests, use in-memory instances or proper test fixtures\n- **Use fake timers** for any code involving `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, or timing-sensitive logic\n\n</system_reminder>\n<system_reminder>\nContents of test/CLAUDE.md:\n\n@AGENTS.md\n\n</system_reminder>","source":"instruction-discovery","interactionId":"ac55048d-6b17-4d27-88b1-3c4becbe46bc"},"id":"87b4e825-7b5b-40a5-8636-9fe2ac47dd06","timestamp":"2026-06-21T21:30:31.234Z","parentId":"07ecfdc5-0a64-4959-b4c0-edb2ab95f2c6"}
